From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF40.248, F41.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I came because... it's airplanes. Just the thought of getting on an airplane right now scares me so bad. Which is crazy, I know it does not make sense. My brother actually works at the airport, he drives one of those little luggage carts. He's been doing it for like fifteen years. He always complains about how fast the tires wear out on the tarmac, especially in July when it gets so hot. You know, we used to try frying eggs on the driveway when it got that hot, back when we were kids. Never really worked, just made a huge mess for my mom to clean up, and she'd get so mad... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, planes. Even though I know in my head it doesn't make sense, my body just tells me that the plane is going to fall. It just feels like it's gonna drop right out of the sky.\n\nDoctor: Is it just airplanes that scare you, or are there other situations that make you feel similarly anxious?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just the airplanes. I mean, I don't love heights or anything, but I can go up in a tall building fine. It's just the flying part. Which is tough because, you know, my wife has been wanting to go to Hawaii for our anniversary. We've been married for almost twenty years now. We met back when I was in my residency, actually. She was a nurse on the cardiac floor. Man, those were some long shifts. I remember one time we had this massive snowstorm, just dumped like three feet of snow overnight, and nobody could leave the hospital. We ended up sleeping on cots in the breakroom for two days. They actually ran out of coffee in the cafeteria, which was a disaster. Have you ever seen a bunch of doctors and nurses without coffee? It's not pretty. \n\nBut yeah, I haven't noticed anything else that makes me feel that way. Just the planes.\n\nDoctor: When did your fear of flying start?\n\nPatient: Well, it started about seven months ago, I'd say. Yeah, right around then. I had just lost my job at the clinic, which was a whole mess on its own. They brought in this new management company, you know how it goes, all these efficiency experts in suits who don't know the first thing about patient care. They started cutting corners everywhere, even changed the brand of gloves we were using to this cheap stuff that would tear half the time you put them on. I brought it up at a staff meeting, just trying to be helpful, and next thing I know, I'm being let go. It was a real blow. \n\nAnyway, right after that happened, I had to take this flight on a small plane to go see my sister. She lives up in the mountains, runs a little bed and breakfast. Beautiful place, actually, she's got these amazing blueberry bushes out back. She makes the best jam. But that flight... man, we hit some turbulence that was just unbelievable. I thought that was it. And ever since that day, about seven months ago, I just can't do it.\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of flying stopped you from doing things you want or need to do, like traveling for work or visiting family and friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it... it really has. I mean, as a physician, you get opportunities to cover shifts in other cities or states, right? Locum tenens work. I've had to turn down all the flight-based coverage, which has definitely cost me a good chunk of income. And honestly, I think my colleagues are starting to wonder if they can even rely on me anymore. Which is frustrating because I've always prided myself on being dependable. Like back when I was a resident, I was the guy who would always take the holiday shifts. I remember one Thanksgiving, I worked a 36-hour shift, and the only turkey I had was this sad, dry sandwich from the vending machine. The machine actually ate my dollar the first time, and I had to shake it to get the sandwich to drop. \n\nBut yeah, I've also missed some really important professional medical conferences because of this. And my family... I've been putting off going to see my parents. They retired down to Florida a few years ago. Bought a nice little condo near the beach. My dad spends all day trying to fix up this old boat he bought off Craigslist. He says he's going to take us all fishing when it's done, but my mom says the thing is a money pit. She's probably right, you know how those old boats are. But yeah, I haven't been able to visit them because the drive is just too long, and I just can't get on a plane.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any anxiety in other situations where you feel a loss of control, besides flying?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. It's really just the planes. I mean, I don't mind not being the one in charge in other parts of my life. Like, I don't get anxious when I'm just a passenger in a car or anything. Which is saying something, because my wife is actually a terrible driver, if I'm being honest. She rides the brakes constantly. We have this older Subaru, and we just had the brake pads replaced, but she somehow makes them squeak anyway. I keep telling her she needs to coast more, but she just ignores me. \n\nWe took it to our mechanic, this guy named Sal down on 4th street, and he said the brakes were perfectly fine. Sal's a great guy, actually, he always gives us a discount because I helped his dad out with some blood pressure management a few years back. He’s got this huge, fat golden retriever that just sleeps right in the middle of the garage floor while he works. You literally have to step over the dog to get to the cash register. I think the dog's name is Barnaby or something like that. Sweetest dog you'll ever meet, but he sheds everywhere. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't get panicky about being out of control in situations like that. It's literally just the flying.\n\nDoctor: How much distress does your fear of flying cause you on a daily basis?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's pretty severe, honestly. It really gets to me. Even if I just get an email from the locum agency about a rural shift that requires a flight, my heart starts pounding right away. My hands get all sweaty, and I feel like I literally cannot breathe until I just close the message and delete it. Which is terrible because I really used to love rural medicine. When I was younger, I did a rotation out in this tiny town in Montana. Population of like, maybe two thousand? It was incredible. The stars out there... you've never seen anything like it. There's no light pollution at all. \n\nI actually stayed in this little cabin that belonged to the town's only other doctor, old Doc Henderson. He was this grumpy guy who always wore suspenders and chewed on unlit cigars. He had this antique stethoscope from the 1950s that he refused to replace, swore it had better acoustics than anything modern. He showed me how to fly-fish on my days off. We'd go down to the river, and I was terrible at it, kept snagging my line in the trees. He'd just laugh and chew his cigar. I actually bought a fly rod when I got back home, thinking I'd take it up as a hobby, but it's just been sitting in my garage for fifteen years gathering dust. I think my wife tried to use it to get a tennis ball out from under the couch once. But yeah, the distress is really bad. Just the thought of having to get on a plane to go anywhere makes me panic.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any other types of anxiety or fear that are not related to flying?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing else really. It's just the planes. I don't get worked up about anything else, honestly. I've always been a pretty even-keeled guy, you know? Like, even in the ER, I'm the one who stays calm when everything goes sideways. I remember this one time, we had a guy come in who had accidentally superglued his hand to his forehead. He was trying to fix a broken coffee mug, apparently. He was panicking, screaming that he was going to be stuck like that forever, and the nurses were trying not to laugh. I just got some acetone and slowly worked it loose. \n\nIt took about forty-five minutes, and we ended up talking about his job. He was a local baker, made these incredible sourdough loaves. Actually, he brought me a loaf a week later as a thank you. Best bread I've ever had. I tried making sourdough during the pandemic, like everyone else did, right? Made my own starter, named it 'Dough-vid'. But I killed it after two weeks because I forgot to feed it. My wife still teases me about it. She says I can keep humans alive but not a jar of flour and water. But anyway, no, I don't have any other fears or anxieties like that. Just the flying.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks in situations other than when you're thinking about or are on an airplane?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. It's really just the airplanes that do it to me. I've never had a panic attack anywhere else. I mean, I guess I had a moment of sheer terror once when my daughter was little and she wandered off at the grocery store, but that's just normal parent stuff, right? \n\nWe were at this massive supermarket, one of those places that sells everything from tires to rotisserie chickens. I turned my back for two seconds to look at the ingredients on a jar of pasta sauce—my wife is super particular about sodium, so I have to check every label. She read this article once about how hidden sodium is ruining our cardiovascular health, and she completely overhauled our pantry. Threw out all my favorite snacks. I used to love those little pretzel nuggets filled with peanut butter, but they didn't make the cut. \n\nAnyway, I turned back around, and my daughter was just gone. I found her about three minutes later in the cereal aisle, trying to climb the shelves to reach a box of something with a cartoon leprechaun on it. She was perfectly fine, but man, my heart was in my throat. But that wasn't a panic attack like with the flying. That was just, you know, losing your kid in a store. So no, no panic attacks outside of the whole airplane thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least six months where you felt anxious or worried most days, even when you weren't thinking about flying?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. I don't really get worried about stuff in general. Like I said, I'm usually pretty level-headed. I mean, my job is stressful, sure. Being a doctor, you see a lot of things, but it doesn't make me anxious day to day. \n\nActually, the only thing that's been stressing me out lately, besides the whole airplane issue, is my neighbor's dog. It's this massive Golden Retriever mix, I think. Beautiful dog, really friendly, but it barks at everything. And I mean everything. The mail carrier, a squirrel, the wind blowing a leaf across the driveway. The guy who owns him, Dave, he's a nice enough guy, works in IT or something, but he just lets the dog out in the yard at like 6:00 AM and leaves him there. \n\nI tried talking to Dave about it once. I brought over some of those cookies my wife makes—the ones with the dark chocolate and sea salt, she uses this specific French butter for them, they're incredible. Anyway, I brought the cookies over, trying to be neighborly, you know? And I mentioned the barking, just casually. He took the cookies, thanked me, and said he'd look into getting a bark collar, but that was three months ago and nothing's changed. I've been thinking about getting one of those ultrasonic birdhouse things that emits a high-pitched noise when a dog barks, but I don't know if they actually work. I read a review online where a guy said it just made his dog bark louder. But anyway, no, I don't have any long-term anxiety or worry like you're talking about. Just the planes.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about having another panic attack in a situation unrelated to flying?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. Like I said, the panic thing is strictly a flying issue. I don't walk around worrying about it happening at the grocery store or at work or anything. I mean, work is busy, but it's a controlled chaos, you know? \n\nSpeaking of work, we actually just got a new espresso machine in the breakroom. One of those fancy Italian ones with the dual boilers and the built-in grinder. The hospital administration finally approved the budget for it after we complained about the drip coffee tasting like battery acid for the last three years. It's a game changer, honestly. I've been watching YouTube tutorials on how to steam milk properly so I can make latte art. I managed to make something that vaguely resembled a fern yesterday, but mostly it just looks like a white blob. One of the newer nurses, Sarah, she used to be a barista in college, and she makes these perfect little swans. It's infuriating how easy she makes it look. I asked her to show me her technique, and she was talking about the angle of the pitcher and the aeration time, and I realized it's basically fluid dynamics. But yeah, no, I don't worry about having panic attacks outside of airplanes. It's just not on my radar.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other areas of your life, besides work and visiting family, where your fear of flying is causing problems or limiting what you can do?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it's definitely causing some real issues, especially with my career. As a doctor, you get these opportunities to do coverage in other places, locum tenens kind of stuff, and I've had to turn down some really lucrative offers because they required flying. It's cost me a significant amount of income. And honestly, it's starting to affect my reputation a bit. Some of my colleagues are starting to wonder if they can really rely on me, you know? Because I always have to be the guy who drives or takes the train, or just bows out entirely. I've missed some major professional medical conferences, too, which is where you do all the important networking. \n\nSpeaking of conferences, the last one I was supposed to go to was in Chicago. I actually really like Chicago. The architecture there is incredible. Have you ever taken that boat tour on the river? The one where they point out all the different styles of buildings? It's fascinating. I went on it years ago with my wife before we had kids. We ate at this deep-dish pizza place afterward, and I swear, the pizza weighed like ten pounds. It was basically a casserole of cheese and tomato sauce. I couldn't even finish one slice. My wife, though, she somehow managed two. She has this crazy metabolism. She runs half-marathons, too, which probably helps. She’s been trying to get me to run with her, but my knees just can't take the pounding on the pavement anymore. I bought a really nice pair of running shoes, those ones with the thick foam soles that are supposed to absorb shock, but they just sit in my closet. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the flying thing. It's holding me back at work, and like I mentioned before, it's really delayed me seeing my family. I haven't seen my parents in almost two years because they live out in California, and the thought of getting on a plane to go see them... I just can't do it. So yeah, it's definitely causing problems.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of flying the only thing that you feel is causing you significant anxiety in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. Just the flying. I mean, like I said, I don't really get anxious about other stuff. It's just that one specific thing. \n\nAlthough, I did have this moment the other day at the grocery store. Not anxiety, really, just... frustration, I guess? I was in the produce section, trying to pick out some avocados. Why is it so impossible to find a good avocado? They're either hard as rocks or total mush. There's like a five-minute window where they're actually ripe. Anyway, I'm standing there squeezing avocados, and this woman next to me is doing the exact same thing, and we just kind of look at each other and sigh. We ended up talking for like ten minutes about guacamole recipes. She puts pomegranate seeds in hers, which sounds weird, but she swore by it. Said it adds a nice crunch and a little sweetness. I might actually try it this weekend if I can find some decent avocados. I usually just stick to the classic—lime juice, cilantro, red onion, a little jalapeño. My kids hate the cilantro, though. They say it tastes like soap. I read somewhere that that's a genetic thing, the soap taste. Isn't that wild? DNA dictating how an herb tastes to you. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, it's just the airplanes. That's the only thing that really gets to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, it's just the flying. Really, that's it. Everything else is fine. I don't have this, like, overarching cloud of anxiety following me around or anything. It's just when planes are involved. 

Which is funny, because you'd think as a doctor, I'd be more anxious about, I don't know, a misdiagnosis or a patient coding on me. But no, I handle all that fine. Actually, speaking of patients, I had this guy come in yesterday with the most bizarre rash. It was all over his forearms, just these perfectly symmetrical red rings. At first, I thought it was ringworm, right? Classic presentation. But he swore he hadn't been around any animals or wrestling mats or anything. Turned out, he'd bought this new, artisanal, organic laundry detergent that was supposedly made entirely out of essential oils and some kind of rare Himalayan tree bark. His skin was just having a massive allergic reaction to it. I told him to just go buy a jug of regular old Tide and take some Benadryl. It's crazy what people will pay for these "natural" products that end up doing more harm than good. My wife is kind of into that stuff, too. She brought home this charcoal toothpaste last week that made my mouth look like I'd been chewing on asphalt. I tried it once and went right back to my Crest. I don't care if the fluoride is bad for my pineal gland or whatever the internet says, I just want clean teeth. 

But anyway, yeah, to answer your question, it's just the flying. No other anxiety stuff going on. Just the planes.

We'd go down to the river, and I was terrible at it, kept snagging my line in the trees. He'd just laugh and chew his cigar. I actually bought a fly rod when I got back home, thinking I'd take it up as a hobby, but it's just been sitting in my garage for fifteen years gathering dust. I think my wife tried to use it to get a tennis ball out from under the couch once. But yeah, the distress is really bad. Just the thought of having to get on a plane to go anywhere makes me panic.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any other types of anxiety or fear that are not related to flying?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing else really. He was trying to fix a broken coffee mug, apparently. He was panicking, screaming that he was going to be stuck like that forever, and the nurses were trying not to laugh. I just got some acetone and slowly worked it loose. \n\nIt took about forty-five minutes, and we ended up talking about his job. He was a local baker, made these incredible sourdough loaves. Just the flying.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks in situations other than when you're thinking about or are on an airplane?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. It's really just the airplanes that do it to me. I've never had a panic attack anywhere else. I actually really like Chicago. The architecture there is incredible. Have you ever taken that boat tour on the river? The one where they point out all the different styles of buildings? It's fascinating. I went on it years ago with my wife before we had kids. We ate at this deep-dish pizza place afterward, and I swear, the pizza weighed like ten pounds. It was basically a casserole of cheese and tomato sauce. I couldn't even finish one slice. My wife, though, she somehow managed two. She has this crazy metabolism. She runs half-marathons, too, which probably helps. She’s been trying to get me to run with her, but my knees just can't take the pounding on the pavement anymore. I bought a really nice pair of running shoes, those ones with the thick foam soles that are supposed to absorb shock, but they just sit in my closet. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the flying thing. It's holding me back at work, and like I mentioned before, it's really delayed me seeing my family. I haven't seen my parents in almost two years because they live out in California, and the thought of getting on a plane to go see them... I just can't do it. So yeah, it's definitely causing problems.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of flying the only thing that you feel is causing you significant anxiety in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. Just the flying. I mean, like I said, I don't really get anxious about other stuff. It's just that one specific thing. \n\nAlthough, I did have this moment the other day at the grocery store. Not anxiety, really, just... frustration, I guess? I was in the produce section, trying to pick out some avocados. Why is it so impossible to find a good avocado? They're either hard as rocks or total mush. There's like a five-minute window where they're actually ripe. Anyway, I'm standing there squeezing avocados, and this woman next to me is doing the exact same thing, and we just kind of look at each other and sigh. We ended up talking for like ten minutes about guacamole recipes. She puts pomegranate seeds in hers, which sounds weird, but she swore by it. Said it adds a nice crunch and a little sweetness. I might actually try it this weekend if I can find some decent avocados. I usually just stick to the classic—lime juice, cilantro, red onion, a little jalapeño. My kids hate the cilantro, though. They say it tastes like soap. I read somewhere that that's a genetic thing, the soap taste. Isn't that wild? DNA dictating how an herb tastes to you. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, it's just the airplanes. That's the only thing that really gets to me.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of flying the main thing that's causing you anxiety, or are there other factors contributing to your overall anxiety level?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's just the flying. Really, that's it. Everything else is fine. I don't have this, like, overarching cloud of anxiety following me around or anything. It's just when planes are involved. \n\nWhich is funny, because you'd think as a doctor, I'd be more anxious about, I don't know, a misdiagnosis or a patient coding on me. But no, I handle all that fine. Actually, speaking of patients, I had this guy come in yesterday with the most bizarre rash. It was all over his forearms, just these perfectly symmetrical red rings. At first, I thought it was ringworm, right? Classic presentation. But he swore he hadn't been around any animals or wrestling mats or anything. Turned out, he'd bought this new, artisanal, organic laundry detergent that was supposedly made entirely out of essential oils and some kind of rare Himalayan tree bark. His skin was just having a massive allergic reaction to it. I told him to just go buy a jug of regular old Tide and take some Benadryl. It's crazy what people will pay for these \"natural\" products that end up doing more harm than good. My wife is kind of into that stuff, too. She brought home this charcoal toothpaste last week that made my mouth look like I'd been chewing on asphalt. I tried it once and went right back to my Crest. I don't care if the fluoride is bad for my pineal gland or whatever the internet says, I just want clean teeth. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, to answer your question, it's just the flying. No other anxiety stuff going on. Just the planes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I came because... it's airplanes. Just the thought of getting on an airplane right now scares me so bad. Which is crazy, I know it does not make sense. My brother actually works at the airport, he drives one of those little luggage carts. He's been doing it for like fifteen years. He always complains about how fast the tires wear out on the tarmac, especially in July when it gets so hot. You know, we used to try frying eggs on the driveway when it got that hot, back when we were kids. Never really worked, just made a huge mess for my mom to clean up, and she'd get so mad... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, planes. Even though I know in my head it doesn't make sense, my body just tells me that the plane is going to fall. It just feels like it's gonna drop right out of the sky.\n\nDoctor: Is it just airplanes that scare you, or are there other situations that make you feel similarly anxious?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just the airplanes. I mean, I don't love heights or anything, but I can go up in a tall building fine. It's just the flying part. Which is tough because, you know, my wife has been wanting to go to Hawaii for our anniversary. We've been married for almost twenty years now. We met back when I was in my residency, actually. She was a nurse on the cardiac floor. Man, those were some long shifts. I remember one time we had this massive snowstorm, just dumped like three feet of snow overnight, and nobody could leave the hospital. We ended up sleeping on cots in the breakroom for two days. They actually ran out of coffee in the cafeteria, which was a disaster. Have you ever seen a bunch of doctors and nurses without coffee? It's not pretty. \n\nBut yeah, I haven't noticed anything else that makes me feel that way. Just the planes.\n\nDoctor: When did your fear of flying start?\n\nPatient: Well, it started about seven months ago, I'd say. Yeah, right around then. I had just lost my job at the clinic, which was a whole mess on its own. They brought in this new management company, you know how it goes, all these efficiency experts in suits who don't know the first thing about patient care. They started cutting corners everywhere, even changed the brand of gloves we were using to this cheap stuff that would tear half the time you put them on. I brought it up at a staff meeting, just trying to be helpful, and next thing I know, I'm being let go. It was a real blow. \n\nAnyway, right after that happened, I had to take this flight on a small plane to go see my sister. She lives up in the mountains, runs a little bed and breakfast. Beautiful place, actually, she's got these amazing blueberry bushes out back. She makes the best jam. But that flight... man, we hit some turbulence that was just unbelievable. I thought that was it. And ever since that day, about seven months ago, I just can't do it.\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of flying stopped you from doing things you want or need to do, like traveling for work or visiting family and friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it... it really has. I mean, as a physician, you get opportunities to cover shifts in other cities or states, right? Locum tenens work. I've had to turn down all the flight-based coverage, which has definitely cost me a good chunk of income. And honestly, I think my colleagues are starting to wonder if they can even rely on me anymore. Which is frustrating because I've always prided myself on being dependable. Like back when I was a resident, I was the guy who would always take the holiday shifts. I remember one Thanksgiving, I worked a 36-hour shift, and the only turkey I had was this sad, dry sandwich from the vending machine. The machine actually ate my dollar the first time, and I had to shake it to get the sandwich to drop. \n\nBut yeah, I've also missed some really important professional medical conferences because of this. And my family... I've been putting off going to see my parents. They retired down to Florida a few years ago. Bought a nice little condo near the beach. My dad spends all day trying to fix up this old boat he bought off Craigslist. He says he's going to take us all fishing when it's done, but my mom says the thing is a money pit. She's probably right, you know how those old boats are. But yeah, I haven't been able to visit them because the drive is just too long, and I just can't get on a plane.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any anxiety in other situations where you feel a loss of control, besides flying?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. It's really just the planes. I mean, I don't mind not being the one in charge in other parts of my life. Like, I don't get anxious when I'm just a passenger in a car or anything. Which is saying something, because my wife is actually a terrible driver, if I'm being honest. She rides the brakes constantly. We have this older Subaru, and we just had the brake pads replaced, but she somehow makes them squeak anyway. I keep telling her she needs to coast more, but she just ignores me. \n\nWe took it to our mechanic, this guy named Sal down on 4th street, and he said the brakes were perfectly fine. Sal's a great guy, actually, he always gives us a discount because I helped his dad out with some blood pressure management a few years back. He’s got this huge, fat golden retriever that just sleeps right in the middle of the garage floor while he works. You literally have to step over the dog to get to the cash register. I think the dog's name is Barnaby or something like that. Sweetest dog you'll ever meet, but he sheds everywhere. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't get panicky about being out of control in situations like that. It's literally just the flying.\n\nDoctor: How much distress does your fear of flying cause you on a daily basis?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's pretty severe, honestly. It really gets to me. Even if I just get an email from the locum agency about a rural shift that requires a flight, my heart starts pounding right away. My hands get all sweaty, and I feel like I literally cannot breathe until I just close the message and delete it. Which is terrible because I really used to love rural medicine. When I was younger, I did a rotation out in this tiny town in Montana. Population of like, maybe two thousand? It was incredible. The stars out there... you've never seen anything like it. There's no light pollution at all. \n\nI actually stayed in this little cabin that belonged to the town's only other doctor, old Doc Henderson. He was this grumpy guy who always wore suspenders and chewed on unlit cigars. He had this antique stethoscope from the 1950s that he refused to replace, swore it had better acoustics than anything modern. He showed me how to fly-fish on my days off. We'd go down to the river, and I was terrible at it, kept snagging my line in the trees. He'd just laugh and chew his cigar. I actually bought a fly rod when I got back home, thinking I'd take it up as a hobby, but it's just been sitting in my garage for fifteen years gathering dust. I think my wife tried to use it to get a tennis ball out from under the couch once. But yeah, the distress is really bad. Just the thought of having to get on a plane to go anywhere makes me panic.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any other types of anxiety or fear that are not related to flying?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing else really. It's just the planes. I don't get worked up about anything else, honestly. I've always been a pretty even-keeled guy, you know? Like, even in the ER, I'm the one who stays calm when everything goes sideways. I remember this one time, we had a guy come in who had accidentally superglued his hand to his forehead. He was trying to fix a broken coffee mug, apparently. He was panicking, screaming that he was going to be stuck like that forever, and the nurses were trying not to laugh. I just got some acetone and slowly worked it loose. \n\nIt took about forty-five minutes, and we ended up talking about his job. He was a local baker, made these incredible sourdough loaves. Actually, he brought me a loaf a week later as a thank you. Best bread I've ever had. I tried making sourdough during the pandemic, like everyone else did, right? Made my own starter, named it 'Dough-vid'. But I killed it after two weeks because I forgot to feed it. My wife still teases me about it. She says I can keep humans alive but not a jar of flour and water. But anyway, no, I don't have any other fears or anxieties like that. Just the flying.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks in situations other than when you're thinking about or are on an airplane?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. It's really just the airplanes that do it to me. I've never had a panic attack anywhere else. I mean, I guess I had a moment of sheer terror once when my daughter was little and she wandered off at the grocery store, but that's just normal parent stuff, right? \n\nWe were at this massive supermarket, one of those places that sells everything from tires to rotisserie chickens. I turned my back for two seconds to look at the ingredients on a jar of pasta sauce—my wife is super particular about sodium, so I have to check every label. She read this article once about how hidden sodium is ruining our cardiovascular health, and she completely overhauled our pantry. Threw out all my favorite snacks. I used to love those little pretzel nuggets filled with peanut butter, but they didn't make the cut. \n\nAnyway, I turned back around, and my daughter was just gone. I found her about three minutes later in the cereal aisle, trying to climb the shelves to reach a box of something with a cartoon leprechaun on it. She was perfectly fine, but man, my heart was in my throat. But that wasn't a panic attack like with the flying. That was just, you know, losing your kid in a store. So no, no panic attacks outside of the whole airplane thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least six months where you felt anxious or worried most days, even when you weren't thinking about flying?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. I don't really get worried about stuff in general. Like I said, I'm usually pretty level-headed. I mean, my job is stressful, sure. Being a doctor, you see a lot of things, but it doesn't make me anxious day to day. \n\nActually, the only thing that's been stressing me out lately, besides the whole airplane issue, is my neighbor's dog. It's this massive Golden Retriever mix, I think. Beautiful dog, really friendly, but it barks at everything. And I mean everything. The mail carrier, a squirrel, the wind blowing a leaf across the driveway. The guy who owns him, Dave, he's a nice enough guy, works in IT or something, but he just lets the dog out in the yard at like 6:00 AM and leaves him there. \n\nI tried talking to Dave about it once. I brought over some of those cookies my wife makes—the ones with the dark chocolate and sea salt, she uses this specific French butter for them, they're incredible. Anyway, I brought the cookies over, trying to be neighborly, you know? And I mentioned the barking, just casually. He took the cookies, thanked me, and said he'd look into getting a bark collar, but that was three months ago and nothing's changed. I've been thinking about getting one of those ultrasonic birdhouse things that emits a high-pitched noise when a dog barks, but I don't know if they actually work. I read a review online where a guy said it just made his dog bark louder. But anyway, no, I don't have any long-term anxiety or worry like you're talking about. Just the planes.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about having another panic attack in a situation unrelated to flying?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. Like I said, the panic thing is strictly a flying issue. I don't walk around worrying about it happening at the grocery store or at work or anything. I mean, work is busy, but it's a controlled chaos, you know? \n\nSpeaking of work, we actually just got a new espresso machine in the breakroom. One of those fancy Italian ones with the dual boilers and the built-in grinder. The hospital administration finally approved the budget for it after we complained about the drip coffee tasting like battery acid for the last three years. It's a game changer, honestly. I've been watching YouTube tutorials on how to steam milk properly so I can make latte art. I managed to make something that vaguely resembled a fern yesterday, but mostly it just looks like a white blob. One of the newer nurses, Sarah, she used to be a barista in college, and she makes these perfect little swans. It's infuriating how easy she makes it look. I asked her to show me her technique, and she was talking about the angle of the pitcher and the aeration time, and I realized it's basically fluid dynamics. But yeah, no, I don't worry about having panic attacks outside of airplanes. It's just not on my radar.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other areas of your life, besides work and visiting family, where your fear of flying is causing problems or limiting what you can do?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it's definitely causing some real issues, especially with my career. As a doctor, you get these opportunities to do coverage in other places, locum tenens kind of stuff, and I've had to turn down some really lucrative offers because they required flying. It's cost me a significant amount of income. And honestly, it's starting to affect my reputation a bit. Some of my colleagues are starting to wonder if they can really rely on me, you know? Because I always have to be the guy who drives or takes the train, or just bows out entirely. I've missed some major professional medical conferences, too, which is where you do all the important networking. \n\nSpeaking of conferences, the last one I was supposed to go to was in Chicago. I actually really like Chicago. The architecture there is incredible. Have you ever taken that boat tour on the river? The one where they point out all the different styles of buildings? It's fascinating. I went on it years ago with my wife before we had kids. We ate at this deep-dish pizza place afterward, and I swear, the pizza weighed like ten pounds. It was basically a casserole of cheese and tomato sauce. I couldn't even finish one slice. My wife, though, she somehow managed two. She has this crazy metabolism. She runs half-marathons, too, which probably helps. She’s been trying to get me to run with her, but my knees just can't take the pounding on the pavement anymore. I bought a really nice pair of running shoes, those ones with the thick foam soles that are supposed to absorb shock, but they just sit in my closet. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the flying thing. It's holding me back at work, and like I mentioned before, it's really delayed me seeing my family. I haven't seen my parents in almost two years because they live out in California, and the thought of getting on a plane to go see them... I just can't do it. So yeah, it's definitely causing problems.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of flying the only thing that you feel is causing you significant anxiety in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. Just the flying. I mean, like I said, I don't really get anxious about other stuff. It's just that one specific thing. \n\nAlthough, I did have this moment the other day at the grocery store. Not anxiety, really, just... frustration, I guess? I was in the produce section, trying to pick out some avocados. Why is it so impossible to find a good avocado? They're either hard as rocks or total mush. There's like a five-minute window where they're actually ripe. Anyway, I'm standing there squeezing avocados, and this woman next to me is doing the exact same thing, and we just kind of look at each other and sigh. We ended up talking for like ten minutes about guacamole recipes. She puts pomegranate seeds in hers, which sounds weird, but she swore by it. Said it adds a nice crunch and a little sweetness. I might actually try it this weekend if I can find some decent avocados. I usually just stick to the classic—lime juice, cilantro, red onion, a little jalapeño. My kids hate the cilantro, though. They say it tastes like soap. I read somewhere that that's a genetic thing, the soap taste. Isn't that wild? DNA dictating how an herb tastes to you. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, it's just the airplanes. That's the only thing that really gets to me.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of flying the main thing that's causing you anxiety, or are there other factors contributing to your overall anxiety level?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's just the flying. Really, that's it. Everything else is fine. I don't have this, like, overarching cloud of anxiety following me around or anything. It's just when planes are involved. \n\nWhich is funny, because you'd think as a doctor, I'd be more anxious about, I don't know, a misdiagnosis or a patient coding on me. But no, I handle all that fine. Actually, speaking of patients, I had this guy come in yesterday with the most bizarre rash. It was all over his forearms, just these perfectly symmetrical red rings. At first, I thought it was ringworm, right? Classic presentation. But he swore he hadn't been around any animals or wrestling mats or anything. Turned out, he'd bought this new, artisanal, organic laundry detergent that was supposedly made entirely out of essential oils and some kind of rare Himalayan tree bark. His skin was just having a massive allergic reaction to it. I told him to just go buy a jug of regular old Tide and take some Benadryl. It's crazy what people will pay for these \"natural\" products that end up doing more harm than good. My wife is kind of into that stuff, too. She brought home this charcoal toothpaste last week that made my mouth look like I'd been chewing on asphalt. I tried it once and went right back to my Crest. I don't care if the fluoride is bad for my pineal gland or whatever the internet says, I just want clean teeth. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, to answer your question, it's just the flying. No other anxiety stuff going on. Just the planes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of flying is something that could be overcome with gradual exposure and practice, or does it feel like it's a part of something deeper?\n\nPatient: I don't know, maybe? Like, practice might help, but it just feels so... big. So overwhelming. I can't even picture myself getting on a plane right now, let alone practicing it. \n\nYou know, speaking of practice, I was trying to teach my youngest how to ride a bike last weekend. We took the training wheels off, and it was a disaster. He just kept tipping over. I told him he just needed to keep practicing, to get a feel for the balance, but he was so frustrated. He threw his helmet in the bushes and refused to get back on. Kids are so funny like that, they want to be perfect at something the very first time they try it. I remember when I was his age, my dad taught me to ride on this old, heavy Schwinn that was way too big for me. I crashed into the neighbor's mailbox and bent the front tire. Dad made me help him fix the mailbox, which took all afternoon. We had to mix concrete and everything. It was actually kind of fun, getting my hands dirty. I don't really do much handy stuff around the house anymore. I usually just hire someone. I tried to fix a leaky faucet last month and ended up flooding the bathroom floor. My wife was not happy. We had to use all our good towels to soak it up. \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? Oh, about the flying. Yeah, I don't know if practice would work. It just feels like this huge wall I can't climb over.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not really. I don't just sit around feeling scared out of nowhere. I mean, sometimes my heart might race a little, but that's usually just because I've had way too much coffee. 

We actually just got this new espresso machine in the doctors' lounge at the clinic. It's one of those overly complicated Italian ones with the pressure dials and the steam wand. Honestly, it's ridiculous. Nobody knows how to use it properly. Dr. Evans—he's one of the senior attendings—he thinks he's some kind of master barista now. He's always back there frothing oat milk or whatever, but he leaves a complete disaster behind him. There are always coffee grounds all over the counter, milk crusted onto the steam wand. It's a biohazard. I ended up just bringing my own little French press from home so I don't have to deal with his messes. I prefer a dark roast anyway. A good Sumatra. 

My brother actually went to Sumatra a couple of years ago. He sent me these amazing pictures of the coffee plantations out there. He's a freelance photographer, so he travels all over the world. Which, you know, good for him, but I couldn't do it. Obviously. Just the thought of getting on all those international flights makes my stomach turn. 

But wait, what were you asking me? Oh, right, feeling anxious for no reason. No, I don't get that at all. It's really just the airplanes. If I'm on the ground, I'm perfectly fine.

Just the planes.\n\nDoctor: When did your fear of flying start?\n\nPatient: Well, it started about seven months ago, I'd say. Yeah, right around then. I had just lost my job at the clinic, which was a whole mess on its own. They brought in this new management company, you know how it goes, all these efficiency experts in suits who don't know the first thing about patient care. They started cutting corners everywhere, even changed the brand of gloves we were using to this cheap stuff that would tear half the time you put them on. I brought it up at a staff meeting, just trying to be helpful, and next thing I know, I'm being let go. It was a real blow. \n\nAnyway, right after that happened, I had to take this flight on a small plane to go see my sister. She lives up in the mountains, runs a little bed and breakfast. Beautiful place, actually, she's got these amazing blueberry bushes out back. She makes the best jam. But that flight... man, we hit some turbulence that was just unbelievable. I thought that was it. And ever since that day, about seven months ago, I just can't do it.\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of flying stopped you from doing things you want or need to do, like traveling for work or visiting family and friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it... it really has. I mean, as a physician, you get opportunities to cover shifts in other cities or states, right? Locum tenens work. I've had to turn down all the flight-based coverage, which has definitely cost me a good chunk of income. And honestly, I think my colleagues are starting to wonder if they can even rely on me anymore. Which is frustrating because I've always prided myself on being dependable. Like back when I was a resident, I was the guy who would always take the holiday shifts. I remember one Thanksgiving, I worked a 36-hour shift, and the only turkey I had was this sad, dry sandwich from the vending machine. The machine actually ate my dollar the first time, and I had to shake it to get the sandwich to drop. \n\nBut yeah, I've also missed some really important professional medical conferences because of this. And my family... I've been putting off going to see my parents. They retired down to Florida a few years ago. Bought a nice little condo near the beach. My dad spends all day trying to fix up this old boat he bought off Craigslist. He says he's going to take us all fishing when it's done, but my mom says the thing is a money pit. She's probably right, you know how those old boats are. But yeah, I haven't been able to visit them because the drive is just too long, and I just can't get on a plane.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any anxiety in other situations where you feel a loss of control, besides flying?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. It's really just the planes. I mean, I don't mind not being the one in charge in other parts of my life. Like, I don't get anxious when I'm just a passenger in a car or anything. Which is saying something, because my wife is actually a terrible driver, if I'm being honest. She rides the brakes constantly. We have this older Subaru, and we just had the brake pads replaced, but she somehow makes them squeak anyway. I keep telling her she needs to coast more, but she just ignores me. \n\nWe took it to our mechanic, this guy named Sal down on 4th street, and he said the brakes were perfectly fine. Sal's a great guy, actually, he always gives us a discount because I helped his dad out with some blood pressure management a few years back. He’s got this huge, fat golden retriever that just sleeps right in the middle of the garage floor while he works. You literally have to step over the dog to get to the cash register. I think the dog's name is Barnaby or something like that. Sweetest dog you'll ever meet, but he sheds everywhere. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't get panicky about being out of control in situations like that. It's literally just the flying.\n\nDoctor: How much distress does your fear of flying cause you on a daily basis?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's pretty severe, honestly. It really gets to me. Even if I just get an email from the locum agency about a rural shift that requires a flight, my heart starts pounding right away. My hands get all sweaty, and I feel like I literally cannot breathe until I just close the message and delete it. Which is terrible because I really used to love rural medicine. When I was younger, I did a rotation out in this tiny town in Montana. Population of like, maybe two thousand? It was incredible. The stars out there... you've never seen anything like it. There's no light pollution at all. \n\nI actually stayed in this little cabin that belonged to the town's only other doctor, old Doc Henderson. He was this grumpy guy who always wore suspenders and chewed on unlit cigars. He had this antique stethoscope from the 1950s that he refused to replace, swore it had better acoustics than anything modern. He showed me how to fly-fish on my days off. We'd go down to the river, and I was terrible at it, kept snagging my line in the trees. He'd just laugh and chew his cigar. I actually bought a fly rod when I got back home, thinking I'd take it up as a hobby, but it's just been sitting in my garage for fifteen years gathering dust. I think my wife tried to use it to get a tennis ball out from under the couch once. But yeah, the distress is really bad. Just the thought of having to get on a plane to go anywhere makes me panic.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any other types of anxiety or fear that are not related to flying?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing else really. It's just the planes. I don't get worked up about anything else, honestly. I've always been a pretty even-keeled guy, you know? Like, even in the ER, I'm the one who stays calm when everything goes sideways. I remember this one time, we had a guy come in who had accidentally superglued his hand to his forehead. He was trying to fix a broken coffee mug, apparently. He was panicking, screaming that he was going to be stuck like that forever, and the nurses were trying not to laugh. I just got some acetone and slowly worked it loose. \n\nIt took about forty-five minutes, and we ended up talking about his job. He was a local baker, made these incredible sourdough loaves. Actually, he brought me a loaf a week later as a thank you. Best bread I've ever had. I tried making sourdough during the pandemic, like everyone else did, right? Made my own starter, named it 'Dough-vid'. But I killed it after two weeks because I forgot to feed it. My wife still teases me about it. She says I can keep humans alive but not a jar of flour and water. But anyway, no, I don't have any other fears or anxieties like that. Just the flying.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks in situations other than when you're thinking about or are on an airplane?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. It's really just the airplanes that do it to me. I've never had a panic attack anywhere else. I mean, I guess I had a moment of sheer terror once when my daughter was little and she wandered off at the grocery store, but that's just normal parent stuff, right? \n\nWe were at this massive supermarket, one of those places that sells everything from tires to rotisserie chickens. I turned my back for two seconds to look at the ingredients on a jar of pasta sauce—my wife is super particular about sodium, so I have to check every label. She read this article once about how hidden sodium is ruining our cardiovascular health, and she completely overhauled our pantry. Threw out all my favorite snacks. I used to love those little pretzel nuggets filled with peanut butter, but they didn't make the cut. \n\nAnyway, I turned back around, and my daughter was just gone. I found her about three minutes later in the cereal aisle, trying to climb the shelves to reach a box of something with a cartoon leprechaun on it. She was perfectly fine, but man, my heart was in my throat. But that wasn't a panic attack like with the flying. That was just, you know, losing your kid in a store. So no, no panic attacks outside of the whole airplane thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least six months where you felt anxious or worried most days, even when you weren't thinking about flying?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. I don't really get worried about stuff in general. Like I said, I'm usually pretty level-headed. I mean, my job is stressful, sure. Being a doctor, you see a lot of things, but it doesn't make me anxious day to day. \n\nActually, the only thing that's been stressing me out lately, besides the whole airplane issue, is my neighbor's dog. It's this massive Golden Retriever mix, I think. Beautiful dog, really friendly, but it barks at everything. And I mean everything. The mail carrier, a squirrel, the wind blowing a leaf across the driveway. The guy who owns him, Dave, he's a nice enough guy, works in IT or something, but he just lets the dog out in the yard at like 6:00 AM and leaves him there. \n\nI tried talking to Dave about it once. I brought over some of those cookies my wife makes—the ones with the dark chocolate and sea salt, she uses this specific French butter for them, they're incredible. Anyway, I brought the cookies over, trying to be neighborly, you know? And I mentioned the barking, just casually. He took the cookies, thanked me, and said he'd look into getting a bark collar, but that was three months ago and nothing's changed. I've been thinking about getting one of those ultrasonic birdhouse things that emits a high-pitched noise when a dog barks, but I don't know if they actually work. I read a review online where a guy said it just made his dog bark louder. But anyway, no, I don't have any long-term anxiety or worry like you're talking about. Just the planes.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about having another panic attack in a situation unrelated to flying?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. Like I said, the panic thing is strictly a flying issue. I don't walk around worrying about it happening at the grocery store or at work or anything. I mean, work is busy, but it's a controlled chaos, you know? \n\nSpeaking of work, we actually just got a new espresso machine in the breakroom. One of those fancy Italian ones with the dual boilers and the built-in grinder. The hospital administration finally approved the budget for it after we complained about the drip coffee tasting like battery acid for the last three years. It's a game changer, honestly. I've been watching YouTube tutorials on how to steam milk properly so I can make latte art. I managed to make something that vaguely resembled a fern yesterday, but mostly it just looks like a white blob. One of the newer nurses, Sarah, she used to be a barista in college, and she makes these perfect little swans. It's infuriating how easy she makes it look. I asked her to show me her technique, and she was talking about the angle of the pitcher and the aeration time, and I realized it's basically fluid dynamics. But yeah, no, I don't worry about having panic attacks outside of airplanes. It's just not on my radar.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other areas of your life, besides work and visiting family, where your fear of flying is causing problems or limiting what you can do?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it's definitely causing some real issues, especially with my career. As a doctor, you get these opportunities to do coverage in other places, locum tenens kind of stuff, and I've had to turn down some really lucrative offers because they required flying. It's cost me a significant amount of income. And honestly, it's starting to affect my reputation a bit. Some of my colleagues are starting to wonder if they can really rely on me, you know? Because I always have to be the guy who drives or takes the train, or just bows out entirely. I've missed some major professional medical conferences, too, which is where you do all the important networking. \n\nSpeaking of conferences, the last one I was supposed to go to was in Chicago. I actually really like Chicago. The architecture there is incredible. Have you ever taken that boat tour on the river? The one where they point out all the different styles of buildings? It's fascinating. I went on it years ago with my wife before we had kids. We ate at this deep-dish pizza place afterward, and I swear, the pizza weighed like ten pounds. It was basically a casserole of cheese and tomato sauce. I couldn't even finish one slice. My wife, though, she somehow managed two. She has this crazy metabolism. She runs half-marathons, too, which probably helps. She’s been trying to get me to run with her, but my knees just can't take the pounding on the pavement anymore. I bought a really nice pair of running shoes, those ones with the thick foam soles that are supposed to absorb shock, but they just sit in my closet. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the flying thing. It's holding me back at work, and like I mentioned before, it's really delayed me seeing my family. I haven't seen my parents in almost two years because they live out in California, and the thought of getting on a plane to go see them... I just can't do it. So yeah, it's definitely causing problems.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of flying the only thing that you feel is causing you significant anxiety in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. Just the flying. I mean, like I said, I don't really get anxious about other stuff. It's just that one specific thing. \n\nAlthough, I did have this moment the other day at the grocery store. Not anxiety, really, just... frustration, I guess? I was in the produce section, trying to pick out some avocados. Why is it so impossible to find a good avocado? They're either hard as rocks or total mush. There's like a five-minute window where they're actually ripe. Anyway, I'm standing there squeezing avocados, and this woman next to me is doing the exact same thing, and we just kind of look at each other and sigh. We ended up talking for like ten minutes about guacamole recipes. She puts pomegranate seeds in hers, which sounds weird, but she swore by it. Said it adds a nice crunch and a little sweetness. I might actually try it this weekend if I can find some decent avocados. I usually just stick to the classic—lime juice, cilantro, red onion, a little jalapeño. My kids hate the cilantro, though. They say it tastes like soap. I read somewhere that that's a genetic thing, the soap taste. Isn't that wild? DNA dictating how an herb tastes to you. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, it's just the airplanes. That's the only thing that really gets to me.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of flying the main thing that's causing you anxiety, or are there other factors contributing to your overall anxiety level?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's just the flying. Really, that's it. Everything else is fine. I don't have this, like, overarching cloud of anxiety following me around or anything. It's just when planes are involved. \n\nWhich is funny, because you'd think as a doctor, I'd be more anxious about, I don't know, a misdiagnosis or a patient coding on me. But no, I handle all that fine. Actually, speaking of patients, I had this guy come in yesterday with the most bizarre rash. It was all over his forearms, just these perfectly symmetrical red rings. At first, I thought it was ringworm, right? Classic presentation. But he swore he hadn't been around any animals or wrestling mats or anything. Turned out, he'd bought this new, artisanal, organic laundry detergent that was supposedly made entirely out of essential oils and some kind of rare Himalayan tree bark. His skin was just having a massive allergic reaction to it. I told him to just go buy a jug of regular old Tide and take some Benadryl. It's crazy what people will pay for these \"natural\" products that end up doing more harm than good. My wife is kind of into that stuff, too. She brought home this charcoal toothpaste last week that made my mouth look like I'd been chewing on asphalt. I tried it once and went right back to my Crest. I don't care if the fluoride is bad for my pineal gland or whatever the internet says, I just want clean teeth. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, to answer your question, it's just the flying. No other anxiety stuff going on. Just the planes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of flying is something that could be overcome with gradual exposure and practice, or does it feel like it's a part of something deeper?\n\nPatient: I don't know, maybe? Like, practice might help, but it just feels so... big. So overwhelming. I can't even picture myself getting on a plane right now, let alone practicing it. \n\nYou know, speaking of practice, I was trying to teach my youngest how to ride a bike last weekend. We took the training wheels off, and it was a disaster. He just kept tipping over. I told him he just needed to keep practicing, to get a feel for the balance, but he was so frustrated. He threw his helmet in the bushes and refused to get back on. Kids are so funny like that, they want to be perfect at something the very first time they try it. I remember when I was his age, my dad taught me to ride on this old, heavy Schwinn that was way too big for me. I crashed into the neighbor's mailbox and bent the front tire. Dad made me help him fix the mailbox, which took all afternoon. We had to mix concrete and everything. It was actually kind of fun, getting my hands dirty. I don't really do much handy stuff around the house anymore. I usually just hire someone. I tried to fix a leaky faucet last month and ended up flooding the bathroom floor. My wife was not happy. We had to use all our good towels to soak it up. \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? Oh, about the flying. Yeah, I don't know if practice would work. It just feels like this huge wall I can't climb over.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt anxious or fearful for no apparent reason, not related to flying or any other specific situation?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't just sit around feeling scared out of nowhere. I mean, sometimes my heart might race a little, but that's usually just because I've had way too much coffee. \n\nWe actually just got this new espresso machine in the doctors' lounge at the clinic. It's one of those overly complicated Italian ones with the pressure dials and the steam wand. Honestly, it's ridiculous. Nobody knows how to use it properly. Dr. Evans—he's one of the senior attendings—he thinks he's some kind of master barista now. He's always back there frothing oat milk or whatever, but he leaves a complete disaster behind him. There are always coffee grounds all over the counter, milk crusted onto the steam wand. It's a biohazard. I ended up just bringing my own little French press from home so I don't have to deal with his messes. I prefer a dark roast anyway. A good Sumatra. \n\nMy brother actually went to Sumatra a couple of years ago. He sent me these amazing pictures of the coffee plantations out there. He's a freelance photographer, so he travels all over the world. Which, you know, good for him, but I couldn't do it. Obviously. Just the thought of getting on all those international flights makes my stomach turn. \n\nBut wait, what were you asking me? Oh, right, feeling anxious for no reason. No, I don't get that at all. It's really just the airplanes. If I'm on the ground, I'm perfectly fine.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not at all. I don't really worry about the day-to-day stuff or, you know, the future. Everything's pretty stable. Work is good, family is good. I mean, my oldest daughter is starting to look at colleges, which is a little stressful financially, but not like... overwhelming worry, you know?

It's like a three-day drive if we take our time, stop at some historical sites. I've always wanted to see Mount Rushmore anyway. My wife thinks I'm crazy for wanting to drive halfway across the country when a flight is only a few hours, but she knows how I am. Plus, road trips are great for family bonding, right? You're all stuck in a car together, you have to talk to each other. Although, the last time we did a long drive, the kids just had their headphones on the entire time staring at their phones. I try to limit their screen time, but it's a losing battle. Half the time I catch myself scrolling through news articles when I should be charting at work. It's a terrible habit. 

But yeah, anyway, what was the question? Oh, worrying about daily life. No, I don't really have that issue. I'm generally a pretty laid-back guy, as long as my feet are on solid ground.
